With regard to who is entitled to this benefit, of course, parents of second and subsequent children for the chosen period, 1 year or 2 years, in the total amount of 12 thousand, also to choose from. Here, too, we give flexibility to parents, because we foresee that, for example, the implementation of the EU directive will result in fathers having 9 weeks extra leave, and those families who decide to use these leave by fathers will be able to reduce their period of taking care capital by just one year and thus gain more money. The situation of each family is different, so we try to design these solutions flexibly so that each family can choose the best option for it. This project also has another very important effect, an effect on local governments. And it comes out very strongly to the expectations of local government. During these several months of work in the Ministry, I received many letters from various local governments, from the presidents of large cities, from local authorities from smaller municipalities asking for a system approach to the issue of maintaining seats in crèches, because today it is a huge burden on local governments. And we know well that there are local governments in Poland that invest very strongly because they can afford it, but there are also those who are in trouble. Ladies and gentlemen, this project is a systematic solution to this problem, because each second and subsequent child receives an additional benefit of PLN 500. That's about as much as the local governments had to do on average to keep their seats. And what is more, I stress, this is very important, we write a legal commitment that 400 PLN for every first child in daycare will be allocated directly to daycare, to which parents will report with the first child. This solves absolutely this age-old problem of caring for day care. And we anticipate that this will give local governments greater interest in creating new places. It's not absolutely true that we don't finance it. Ever since the program was extended... And this funding will continue to be maintained. Moreover, it will be increased from next year thanks to EU funds, both to date under the ESF Fund and to the new ones that will come from the National Reconstruction Plan, which I hope will soon be accepted. And that will mean triple the spending on day care, including this system support for maintaining places in daycare, I hope that it will encourage local authorities to invest in day care. However, please remember that we are approaching, in terms of this number of 200,000 seats, to a certain saturation, you can call it that, demand. Of course, I know that in many municipalities there are no crèches at all, and I think that this will make them. However, there are many places where we already see that these seats are available today. The pandemic has shown us how fragile supply chains are, how easy it is to disrupt trade between continents or stop production. That is why already large global companies decide to move their business centres to our region in order to be close to consumers from Western Europe. Around the big business will create a whole ecosystem of discounts for companies, which thanks to big orders will be able to grow quickly, grow from small to medium, and from medium to large. This is a great opportunity for Poland, but also for our neighbours. None of the countries in our region are waiting passively for major international investments. On the contrary, all countries are fighting to get investors to choose them. They are fighting to ensure that domestic companies benefit as much as possible from the emergence of big players on the market. We also want to join this race and we have a chance to win it. That is why the Ministry of Finance has prepared a tax package for the restart of the economy. These are comprehensive tax changes. Their effect will be an incentive for big global players to bet on Poland and significantly facilitate the development of small and medium Polish business. How exactly are we gonna do that? First of all, we will create from the beginning of next year conditions improving the attractiveness of Poland as a place of development of companies, regulations which will attract to us human and financial capital. We will encourage, convince to Poland investors who will choose us to locate companies and create new jobs. We will also invite our compatriots to return to the Vistula River. We'll offer them favorable conditions to return and invest their money. We will also create a friendly climate for the development of family businesses. Secondly, we will implement tax solutions that will directly convince Poland of the great business. Special service for strategic partners, simplified clearing procedures, as well as flexible reinvestment rules and flexibility of investment portfolio development - this is our proposal for those who will now decide whether to place their investments in Poland or in another country of our region. Thirdly, we will make it easier for companies to invest in innovation and productivity, industry 4.0, i.e. in those sectors of the economy that have the highest potential and generate the highest added value. We will create incentives to create new technologies on the Vistula River and to implement new products. We will support the modernisation of production and investment in workers. It is about investing specifically, directly in them, in their new skills, but also about providing them with modern tools to increase their productivity. Fourthly, we will help companies to acquire new markets and in Poland and abroad. We will create favorable conditions to join forces between businesses, some of which are looking for capital and others want to locate their resources in promising companies. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The first tax segment of the part of the Polish government, the tax restart of the economy, is an investment in innovative business. We want every stage of the production process located in Poland to involve tax facilitations. The first one is work on the idea for a new product. There's already an R &amp; D relief there. The second is the translation of the idea into the language of practice. From next year it will be dedicated to the relief for the prototype. Further, at the time of start-up of business production, it should support the relief for robotics and the relief for the employment of strategic and innovative workers. Finally, the lower rate should be taxed income from sales of invented and manufactured goods in Poland. This can be achieved thanks to the ease of IP Box. Therefore, from next year all these solutions will not compete with each other, but, on the contrary, in parallel with each other, reinforce each other's actions. The second segment is to support the development and expansion of companies. With the inflow of capital to Poland, big investments before small and medium-sized businesses will open up new opportunities for development. There will be investors, and with them big contracts. We want companies in the SME sector to benefit as much as possible from new opportunities on the market. We know that in order to do this, they will have to invest in development and their potential. For this they will simply need money and therefore it is necessary to build a tax system that will be investment-friendly, will create a friendly climate around them. That is why we are creating solutions that will effectively encourage business to invest profits. First of all, it will be reliefs supporting the entry of Polish companies to the stock exchange and reliefs operating on the other hand, which will encourage investors to invest in Polish companies, in Polish start-ups either directly or through venture capital funds. We also provide for tax reliefs that will facilitate the restructuring of companies, which is often the key to improving the efficiency of business operations. Companies should also search effectively for new markets so that their growth is not limited by domestic demand. Here, relief will help to support their domestic and foreign expansion. Finally, for companies that want to reduce their tax costs to the maximum, we envisage significant changes in Estonian CIT. Estonian CIT means no tax accounting, 0% tax as long as the company does not pay its shareholders profits. The introduction of the new Estonian CIT, Estonian CIT 2.0, means a common simplification. Its application will be possible not only for limited liability and public limited liability companies, but also for limited limited liability companies, limited limited liability companies, private limited liability companies and cooperatives, regardless of their size. A novelty is the lack of link between the use of Estonian CIT and the obligation to make investments in the coming years. It is a lack of tax on entry and exit and, most importantly, a significant reduction in the rate of tax. The small CIT-ship, i.e. the one earning revenue of up to EUR 2 million per year by paying the dividend, will pay only 20% of the combined CIT and PIT, which is almost as much as it would pay as a self-employed PIT itself. And this, let's remember, without the need to pay the health contribution. So it is one of the most attractive ways of taxing in general functioning in the Polish legal system. The third segment is dedicated to large investors. You might ask yourself: How do you convince Elona Musk to invest in Poland, to conquer space in cooperation with Polish small and medium-sized business? The big business needs three things: rapid information about the amount of taxes it will pay from the planned investment, as little formalities as possible in tax settlement and low taxation. And that's what we're giving to the big business in this project. Anyone who plans a big investment in Poland: whether a Polish company or a foreign investor, will be given the possibility of obtaining interpretation No 590 from the Minister of Finance. It is one decision, one window, one document that will cover all tax consequences of investments - PIT, CIT, VAT, excise duty, even customs or local taxes, one iron letter and 100% Security in contact with the IRS. Secondly, we want holding companies to be as cheap as possible. Therefore, from next year on, companies belonging to one owner will not have to account for VAT among themselves. Poland will join the 18 countries of the European Union that have introduced tax groups in the field of VAT. Moreover, we will abolish the unfavourable rules for the distribution of profits from Poland to countries outside the Union thanks to the Polish holding company - a new institution as regards income tax. Companies from the United States, Japan or China will be able to invest more effectively in Poland without intermediaries from Malta or Luxembourg. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The last segment of our strategy is the repatriation of capital. There is no question of the rapid development of small and medium-sized business without increasing the development potential of Poland. You can ask again what the Polish economy needs. Two things: people and money. Nearly 20 million Poles live abroad. Imagine what would happen if every tenth of them returned to Poland. That is why we offer our compatriots a package of incentives to return to the country with skills, experiences and capital acquired abroad. We offer workers and small business a relief to return, i.e. PIT/0 for returners. Entrepreneurs who have achieved success abroad will be encouraged to move to Poland where to manage their business. We give them favourable rules on the settlement of profits from the interests they conduct in other countries. In turn to the celebrities of culture and sport, our soccer snipers, the shooters' kings we offer favorable conditions for the settlement of sponsorship activities. Ladies and gentlemen! It is up to you whether Poland joins the global race for big investments and whether we will create a tax ecosystem in Poland, which will enable Polish business to take advantage of the opportunities that appear before it. In presenting the above, I kindly ask the High House to adopt the draft law as proposed by the government. Thank you. [[Applause]]

[[There's something else to be done about it.]] Do we want him to support investment so he can support innovation so he can support expansion into other markets? [[We do, and you do everything backwards.]] Madam, please don't disturb me. Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Finance Jan Sarnowski: Absolutely, we all want that, and that's why we're working in both directions. Ladies and gentlemen! The year 2020 and 2021 are tax years - now there is a change within the Polish Government - in which we create at every stage of the company's operation the opportunity to benefit from a simplified method of settlement. Since January this year practically any micro-entrepreneur may benefit from a lump sum settlement from registered revenues. We have eliminated more than 50 exemptions and raised the limit on the use of a lump sum up to eight times, to EUR 2 million of annual income. This means that any micro-entrepreneur who wants to settle himself in a simplified manner can do so without any restrictions. Secondly, the changes that we are making in the field of Estonian CIT from January next year will make it possible for each business, including the larger one, which has a transparent, simple organisational structure, to choose the simplest way of settling the non-payment of taxes on a monthly, quarterly or annual basis, and the tax is calculated in a very simple way: payment times the rate only and only when the profit is paid from the company. Ladies and gentlemen! There are questions about whether the reliefs, the solutions we propose, will be used by entrepreneurs. Examples of R &amp; D relief were set up. Ladies and gentlemen, entrepreneurs will be familiar with these regulations, and from year to year they are increasingly benefiting from research and development relief. In the first year 500 entrepreneurs also benefited, now, after 3 years, there are as many as five times more. The amount of deduction increased as much as tenfold over the five years, from PLN 200 million to PLN 2.5 billion. These expenses would not have been incurred as investment expenditure if no such relief had occurred. As a result, additional funds in the company's budgets could be transferred, transferred to investments, to increase the scale of business carried out by the companies. Ladies and gentlemen! Asking for a health contribution. Poland is a European phenomenon. A modern tax system is a system where the difference between people doing the same work on the basis of different types of contracts is as small as possible. And it is this law that is probably the first in very, very many years, which causes a huge step forward in equalizing this difference, and let us remember that we are talking not only about statistics and Excel, but also about very real problems. Let's see, for example. for fixed-term contracts. In Poland 14% persons employed are employed on the basis of future contracts. This represents as much as half, 50%, more than the European average, that is three times more than in other countries of our region, in the Czech Republic, Slovakia or Hungary. It is worse only in Spain and Portugal, but these are tourist countries, in which the seasonality of some jobs actually dictates that people are employed for fixed-term contracts. Changes in the tax wedge, changes in the amount of the health contribution and above all in the amount of the free amount and the second tax threshold are a huge step towards ensuring that there are no such differences and that there is no such situation. Remember: a person earning 100,000. PLN per year health contributions pay... How much is it? In the case of an employee 7,8 thousand. zł, in the case of an entrepreneur of 4.5 thousand and health care services in both cases are exactly the same. Health contribution in relation to income: up to 100,000 - 8% Up to 500,000. zł - 4% up to PLN 1 million - 2%. These are interest rates for entrepreneurs. The employee is always 9%. This is a fair, modern tax system? No. But it will be better, definitely better as a result of the changes we are making. I would also like to reassure you. The project of the Polish Government does not foresee the liquidation of the tax card. The tax card will continue to function for 100,000. those who now account for themselves in the most simplified way, and no new person will be able to enter this clearing system from January next year. This is a tax treatment which does not in any way relate either to the income or to the income of the person who benefits from this arrangement. In this way, it does not refer to its actual payment capacity either. So that this is a way of taxation that is worth walking away from, but from which it is necessary to move away in an evolutionary way, with the rights acquired on the part of those who benefit from this solution. As regards local authorities, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to remind you once again that not further than yesterday, the High Chamber was discussing changes concerning the financing of local government units with a positive budgetary effect for them, compensating for the loss that could result from the provisions of the Polish Government. The provisions of the Polish Government cause a decrease between PLN 11 and 12 billion in the budgets of local government units, while the changes already under discussion by the High Chamber will lead to the average revenue of local government units growing by around 4,3% year by year. This is a new promotional investment subsidy and, on the other hand, a departure from the close link between the income of local government units and the state of the entities, and the income tax paid by these entities in a specific year. It's about putting it together for three years. It cannot also be that local government units are hostage to the current situation of entities operating in their territory. When a large enterprise operating in a given municipality makes significant investment expenditure, this immediately affects the finances of local government units. He doesn't have to be in any financial trouble. All you have to do is make a completely rational, necessary and constructive business decision. The changes that we are proposing will address this problem and will make shaping the budgets of local government units much simpler and safer. Finally, I would like to thank you very much for your question about the investment agreement. This is a solution dedicated to both Polish and foreign investors, who would like to know all the tax consequences of their investment in Poland. This is one decision that combines five tools that already function: binding price agreement, individual interpretation, binding joint information, binding excise information and a protective opinion. Starting a large business project, you will not have to ask for five different types of interpretations, while they can be of any kind several, so that in total such documents, such materials may need even a dozen. One investment agreement is enough. Here the limit is only and only the amount of the planned investment, i.e. PLN 100 million. We also want this solution to be more and more accessible. So that this limit will also be reduced in the coming years. In three years' time, it'll be reduced by half to 50 million. We very much want as many entrepreneurs as possible to benefit from this tool, because it is practically a new standard, not only in our region, but also across Europe. This service is already offered by investors and domestic, and foreign Italy, Hungary, Slovakia. I am very pleased that from January next year Poland will also join the catalogue of countries that comprehensively support investors and foreign investors and those whose economic situation is so complicated that they need one such solution, one interpretation. If I haven't answered any questions, we will, of course, answer them in writing. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

When building a guaranteed housing loan instrument, the bill assumes that solving the lack of own resources when applying for mortgage credit should not increase the risk of excessive relaxation of credit standards and that the mechanisms adopted eliminate the barrier of lack of own contribution required to borrow a mortgage. They should promote the maintenance of high-quality credit portfolios in the banking sector and should not risk stimulating speculative activities and social attitudes resulting in unjustified increases in housing prices. They should not lead to excessive debt opportunities for households. In view of the above, the provisions of the proposed law lay down the terms and conditions for the granting and repayment of a guaranteed residential loan, including the conditions to be met by a mortgage which is a loan without an own contribution, the scope of the objectives for which a guaranteed residential loan may be granted - the purchase of a dwelling or a single-family house, the construction contribution or the financing of the construction costs of a single-family house - the minimum repayment period of a guaranteed residential loan of 15 years, the conditions to be met by the property to be financed, the maximum price limit, including the building contribution per 1 m With a view to further consistent improvement of the living rate of Polish families in response to demographic challenges, the Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice will support the draft law proposed by the Government of the Republic of Poland. If I do not hear opposition, I will think that the Sejm has accepted the offer. I can't hear you objecting. We proceed with the examination of item 26. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on countering unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in the marketing of agricultural and food products (Print No 1502). I would like to ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, Mr Richard Bartosik, to present the reasons for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u208	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a law against unfair exploitation of contract advantages in the marketing of agricultural and food products. The draft law aims to implement into the national legal order Directive 2019/633 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 April 2019 on unfair commercial practices between traders in the supply chain of agricultural and food products. It should be noted that since 12 July 2017 there have been provisions in force in Poland to prevent unfair exploitation of the advantage of contracts in the marketing of agricultural and food products. Poland as one of the first Member States introduced such legislation before regulations were adopted at EU level. Since Directive 2019/633 introduces provisions beyond the scope of our existing law, it has been decided to draw up a new law, but with the same title. The draft law sets out the principles and procedures for countering unfair practices using the contractual advantage and the principles and manner taken in the interest of the public to protect the interests of suppliers and buyers. In relation to the existing provisions, the draft law introduces a wider scope of competence of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. The extension of the definition of agricultural and food products will also allow for an examination of the accounts relating to buying-in, inter alia. feed, live animals, seeds, oil fruits produced by farmers. The buyer definition has also changed. In the proposed law, the purchaser is an entrepreneur but also a public body which directly or indirectly acquires agricultural or food products from the supplier. According to the draft law, the competent authority for practices involving unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage is, as so far, the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. The tasks arising from the Act will be carried out by the President of the UOKiK using the provisions of the Act of 16 February 2007 on the protection of competition and consumers. The President of the Office shall initiate proceedings in cases of unfair practices using the contractual advantage of the applicant, which shall give protection against the applicant's identification of the notification. The Act thus ensures the anonymity of the applicant party. The provisions of the proposed law allow proceedings to be conducted by the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection not only in the case of unfair practices indicated directly in the Act, but also in situations where an identified practice which has a bearing on the use of a contractual advantage is contrary to good practices and threatens or infringes the essential interests of the other party. High Seym! The draft law lays down penalties for unfair practices using a contractual advantage, for obstructing proceedings and for not making a decision imposing a penalty. The Directive introduces a common minimum framework within the European Union to combat unfair commercial practices in the food supply chain. The proposed law introduces provisions beyond the scope of the Directive. An additional prohibited, unfair practice is the unjustified reduction of claims on the delivery of products after their adoption, in particular as a result of the request for a discount, protection shall also apply to purchasers, as is already the case in the current national legislation, by virtue of the Act in force so far, and unfair commercial practice shall also be contrary to or adversely affecting the legitimate interest of the other party. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed law is one of a number of initiatives aimed at increasing transparency and improving the functioning of the market chain in the agri-food sector and strengthening the bargaining position of farmers in the context of a rapid concentration of trade and processing so as to ensure a fair sharing of the benefits associated, inter alia. with growing exports of Polish products. I would also like to stress that the law is, as in few countries, separate and applies to unfair practices in the agri-food sector. These unfair practices, as I said before, are 16, including 10 completely banned. That's so-called. Black practices. Besides, there's six so-called. grey practices which are permitted but provided that they are clearly and unambiguously agreed in the contract between the buyer and the supplier prior to their application. Ladies and gentlemen! The Directive provided for the adoption of national measures by 1 May of this year and required that the provisions of the proposed law should apply from 1 November of that year. If the High Sejm decides to adopt this law, so will it, these regulations will enter into force on 1 November. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]] [[The meeting is chaired by Deputy Marshal of the Sejm Włodzimierz Czarzasty]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u209	Thank you very much, Minister. Hello, ladies and gentlemen. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and three-minute statements on behalf of the circles. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u210	Thank you kindly, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, it is my pleasure to present to you the position regarding the Government's draft law against unfair exploitation of contract advantages in the marketing of agricultural and food products, print No 1502. The proposal is intended to implement in the national legal order Directive (EU) of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 April 2019 on unfair commercial practices between traders in the supply chain of agricultural and food products. The structure of the agri-food and food market highlights unfair commercial practices between suppliers and buyers of agricultural and food products throughout the food supply chain. Such practices are observed in almost all countries of the European Union. In view of the presence of strong and economically consolidated distribution sector entities, and often even processing against weaker and fragmented operators producing agricultural raw materials and foodstuffs on the market, there are unfair commercial practices applied by these operators, which often leads to the exclusion of weaker operators from the market. Good relations between all actors are essential for the proper functioning of the food supply chain. Directive 2019/633 of 17 April 2019 provides a specific framework for the application of a uniform approach to unfair practices in the food chain. The recommended solution is the adoption of a law to counter unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in the marketing of agricultural and food products. On the other hand, the implementation significantly changes the scope of the 2016 Act on countering the unjustified exploitation of the contractual advantage in trade in agricultural and food products. In relation to the existing provisions, the draft law introduces new amendments to extend the competences of the UOKiK as well as to extend the definition of agricultural and food products, to change the definition of the buyer, to define unfair practices, to identify disparities in economic potential or to introduce a voluntary penalty procedure. The proposed law is intended to ensure more transparent conditions for operators in the marketing of agricultural and food products. Suppliers, including farmers and small and medium-sized enterprises producing agricultural and food products, will have greater certainty on the functioning of the Community market, including on the domestic market, which should translate into promotional measures, including investment and innovation in relation to these socio-professional groups. The proposed law assumes many positive solutions. The law goes beyond the EU directive and is a solution that we recommend to adopt. Nor is the proposed law intended to increase expenditure from the state budget or budgets of local government units. Since implementation into the national legal order of Directive (EU) 2019/633 of the European Parliament and of the Council is not possible by other means, the PiS Parliamentary Club supports the draft Government Print Act No 1502. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u212	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Civic Coalition, I would like to present a position on the adoption of a law against unfair exploitation of the advantage of contracts in trade in agricultural and food products. It so happens that this problem is a pan-European problem and not only concerns the Polish sphere, so the European Parliament and the Council of the European Union adopted the Directive of 17 April 2019 on unfair commercial practices between traders in the supply chain for agricultural and food products. It can be seen that very often the concentration of capital in the case of entities that negotiate from a position of force with suppliers, such as farmers or other agricultural producers, disturbs really normal competition in the single market, disturbs it also in Poland, and therefore it is necessary to regulate at European level so that a common framework, a common minimum, is treated in a similar way in all countries. It so happens that this directive sets a minimum for certain restrictions and a catalogue of unfair practices, but, as we know, this law also introduces additionally protective elements for farmers, of which we are pleased. It has been well consulted and will replace the existing 2016 law. We will support the extension of the definition of agricultural and food products as regards enlargement issues, feedingstuffs, live animals, seeds and oil fruits, which are and will be the subject of farmers' production, as well as the regulation of issues related to the change in the definition of buyers, as well as the extension of matters relating to traders or public authorities in the area of public procurement, which are purchasers, in order to reduce the risk of unfair practices in these relations as well. The proposed law defines 17 categories of unfair practices, including 11 strictly prohibited and six, which are permitted, provided that they are clearly presented and clearly agreed in the contract between the buyer and the supplier. The Act also specifies what is a significant imbalance in economic potential in the case of unfair practices, the exploitation of the buyer's contractual advantage vis-à-vis the supplier, and vice versa. There are also issues of monetary penalties. In fact, we would like to discuss the proposed law and the directive, because we know that a list of unfair behaviour, mainly linked to an unjustified termination or a threat of termination, has been established in the current - still existing - rules. The parties often granted one-sided right to terminate the contract, often made the conclusion of the contract subject to acceptance or fulfilment of additional benefits which were outside the scope of the contract. There was also an excessive extension of payment deadlines. Surely the bill is needed. This would not be a directive if there were no specific abuse. In the end, it is primarily the interest of the farmer and the producer to protect the interests of farmers throughout the supply chain and to reduce this supply chain as much as possible. After all, all this has already been defined, also in the strategy from field to table. As the Civic Coalition, we will support these solutions, but above all we will hope that this law will be well promoted among contractors, buyers, suppliers. The aim is also to create a catalogue of good practice and have good practice in the application of this law and good information about tools that are used to protect especially farmers throughout the supply process. We can count on our full support, because the interests of farmers and producers are the most important in this regard. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u215	Thank you, madam. I invite Mr Mirosław Maliszewski, Parliamentary Club of the Coalition Poland. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u216	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! A few days ago, at the headquarters of one of the largest retail chains in Poland, there was a protest of farmers under the slogan "Stop of a bully in the apple trade." This word "crusher," which at the time was very frequent, refers to practices that today are applied both to those who supply fruit to the retail chain and to all other producers who trade their food products through these networks. The practice is that the supplier is in principle not a partner in the process of agreeing delivery conditions and networks, using its contractual advantage on the market, i.e. their great monopolistic or paramonopolistic power, forcing the suppliers to adverse conditions primarily in the form of low delivery prices. The low delivery prices then translate into farmers who, as the last link of this chain, the weakest link, bear the costs of the rogue operation of many commercial networks in Poland. This phenomenon applies not only to our country but also to other countries of the European Union. Indeed, in 2019. The European Parliament and the Council adopted a directive imposing an obligation on Member States to adopt provisions to prevent or eliminate this practice. What happened that the European Commission and the European Parliament adopted such legislation, such solutions? Well, many of the analyses that were carried out at the time say that in the long term the prices obtained by the manufacturer are stable - on the chart we can imagine this as a horizontal line - or slightly falling. On the other hand, the prices that the consumer pays for the products purchased from a farmer at low prices are rising rapidly, i.e. the line is a rising line. The whole space between the price line for the producer and the price line paid by the consumer shall be filled by the margin of large commercial networks. This, of course, raised huge opposition from European officials, European politicians, which also raises huge opposition from us in Poland, because it is, in our opinion, an unfair use of the contractual advantage to impose low prices on suppliers, and to offer high prices to consumers and raise huge capital. The facts show that this is particularly the case for agricultural, agri-food products due to the specific nature of trade in these products. They reach the market on a seasonal basis, because this is the most common production of agricultural products, e.g. fruits, and even especially fruits, because they are broken at a certain time, e.g. right now. This shop policy leads to price formation for the following months in which the products in question will be sold at low prices. This protest of the Sadists under one of the largest commercial networks in Poland, perhaps the biggest, was intended to prevent this and to draw attention to the public, but also to the government, the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, that this phenomenon must be countered because it wants to develop permanently, it wants to dominate the market. Secondly, these are products that break easily, so it is easy to force suppliers to set a delivery schedule, and it is easy to force prices, because there is virtually no way to store and dispose of this product at longer intervals. And finally, the most important thing that is crucial in this case is the imbalance of partners. Large players, with strong capital, but above all with strong market power, force those smaller, small, weak capital entities which have a small market share and which are dispersed, which are very large, to supply at low prices. These entities absolutely cannot resist such practice because they depend on the recipient, which is a large network. Indeed, the Law is an attempt to organize or prevent this system. This can, of course, be done in two stages. One stage in this law is included, i.e. Certain practices which cannot appear in the contract between the supplier and the recipient of the product shall be prohibited and, when such practices take place, the Office of Competition and Consumer Protection shall intervene at its request or at the request of the entity which is being used. Let us assume that this entity will never submit such a proposal under threat of being expelled from the supplier group. So, in fact, there is another step that unfortunately is not included in this law and which needs to be taken into account. The position of this weak player at the price negotiation stage must be strengthened, i.e. it must be stronger at the price negotiation stage. It is not possible for small, small, fragmented entities, of which there are dozens or dozens, to reach an agreement, because there are too many to reach an agreement, and the law forbids such an agreement (the Bell), so that further work on this project must take account of this specificity, identify or increase the catalogue of prohibited acts at the negotiation stage of the agreement, and at the negotiation stage the conditions for the delivery of products will support those who will negotiate with large commercial networks. Our club in this consultation will actively participate in this work by submitting amendments, because it is a hugely important and huge problem which is of great importance for the income of Polish farmers. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

I would like to inform you that in Poland since July 2017 the provisions of the Act of 15 December 2016 on the prevention of unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in trade in agricultural and food products have already been in force. According to them, unfair exploitation of the buyer's contractual advantage vis-à-vis the supplier and supplier vis-à-vis the buyer is prohibited. The competent authority for unfair practices using the contractual advantage shall be the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u250	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I submit a draft law amending the excise duty refund Act contained in the price of diesel used for agricultural production. This project aims to increase the limit of diesel consumption per hectare of agricultural crops from 100 l to 110 l, as well as for one large cattle conversion unit - from 30 l to 40 l. The increase in financial assistance to agricultural producers in the form of excise duty reimbursement is justified by the need to increase the competitiveness of Polish agricultural holdings on the EU market. The amendments proposed in the Act will contribute to reducing production costs on agricultural holdings by increasing financial support for fuel used in agricultural production. In recent years, agricultural production costs, including fuel use, have increased. The increase in production costs makes agricultural production less profitable. The share of fuel costs in the total costs of agricultural production increases from year to year. This is also due to technical progress, e.g. related to the breeding of bovine animals. It is also estimated that this is related to technologies, often to increased production intensity and the pursuit of better economic performance, as well as to increased fuel prices on the world market, especially during periods of the highest fuel demand in agriculture, mainly during increased field work, particularly harvest and autumn work. More protective treatments or specialized treatments involve appropriate equipment and automatically increase fuel consumption. Intensification of plant and animal production in small family farms and large-scale farms requires the use of modern technologies. The share of fuel consumption in field and transport works and fuel consumption of machinery on the farm is increasing. Increasing quality requirements and technological standards in animal production also contribute to increasing demand for energy carriers for heating and lighting purposes or for propulsion of machinery and equipment. On the other hand, for large-scale farms producing milk at a nutritional dose of cows, highly-intensive feeds, such as silage, hayseeds, have a large share. Such an animal feeding technology is directly linked to the increased cost of fuel at the total cost of maintaining cows. In view of the above, the Polish Łada was included among others. Action under the name: an even greater refund of excise duty on agricultural fuel for which an increase in the consumption limit for diesel used in agricultural production has been established. Ladies and gentlemen! In advance of any questions, I would like to stress that an increase in the rate, an increase in the limit of 1 ha is not the first increase in support for farmers, because there have already been several such increases under the rule of law and justice. I also want to make it clear that this is not the last increase, because we will respond to what is happening on the market. Luckily, the prices of these fuels haven't been so dynamic lately. We have no major crises in world markets. Therefore, we react when needed. If necessary, the government will present another project to increase this agricultural production limit for farmers, the agricultural fuel excise duty limit. Also the issue of households, the amendment of the Law on the monitoring and control of fuel quality, the regulation on quality requirements for fuels is an example of how, while also caring for workers, we take care of the economic situation, care for the environment and clean up the market for individual customers. The restructuring company of mines, which has its role in this process, simultaneously conducts the process of decommissioning inactive mining plants, remediation, drainage, thus saving and the environment, and those urbanized areas where the extraction took place. From the security point of view of underground workers - this is always the most important issue - in 2016 and 2017 legal acts were issued, which, by regulating this issue, reduced the operating costs of companies by nearly PLN 0.5 billion per year. Today's amendment of the Act is part of the continuation of these processes, which I have said here, because it extends the possibility to dispose of mines for SRK by the end of 2023 and thus extends this aid instrument, notified to the public aid instrument in the form of extraordinary costs, and to the liquidation of assets, and to cover, until the end of 2023. On the other hand, it is already part of the implementation of the social contract. These solutions, which we propose in this law, are very beneficial for miners, are agreed with them and I think that the whole House will today acknowledge that they are worth it in this socially agreed form of miners handed over or launched for the currently decommissioned individuals. In practice, this means that we finance mining holidays, holidays for employees of the coal processing plant and one-off cash checks as part of extraordinary costs. One-off cash check - and that's the part of the social contract - that's the amount of 120 thousand. zł. That's exactly what the miner's gonna get on his hand, by the way. As far as mining leave is concerned, those previous mining leave, the workers who are going on this holiday, will receive 80% monthly salary calculated as salary for vacation leave. Yeah, well, in short, it looks like it's in this bill. It's not an extensive act. This, of course, has an impact on the budget and increases the expenditure limit, which has been entered in the State budget, to the amount of 8245 million. These tasks, which we are proposing today, related to the liquidation of assets and shields for exactly 3719 workers who will leave the mining industry with the units being liquidated, are the amount of just over PLN 2 billion: employee leave for 1371 people - approx. 1 billion, one-off cash checks for 2348 people - approx. PLN 164 million, physical liquidation of the mining assets - PLN 669 million, repair of mining damages - PLN 194 million, compensatory pensions - approx. PLN 1 million. The remaining amount of this limit I was talking about is the continuation of the decommissioning tasks that SRK is carrying out. Continuation of these tasks. We know that these are long-term processes and these processes should continue. I don't think there's any doubt about that. Within this limit there are, of course, holidays, and physical liquidation, to the greatest extent, because it is about 4.5 billion PLN, another 0.5 billion is mining damage, and finally the coal equivalent I was talking about is almost 1 billion PLN. That requires notification. We'll make this notification after the arrangements. We are already after the agreement on the government and the approval of the president of NFOŚ, we needed to update the program. The UOKiK currently translates all documents. They are about to be sent to the European Commission. I do not see any reason to be concerned about the notification of this law, i.e. this solution extending the aid instrument in the form of exceptional costs. At the same time, this will probably be discussed and questions will be raised. With the European Commission, we are prenoticed before the new law, which will change the rules on state aid by adding a new instrument - that is, after negotiations with the social side of the European Commission - a subsidy to reduce production, introducing operational programmes for individual individuals and degressive production decreasing in time by 2049. We are at a prenotification stage, i.e. such an informal stage of discussions with the European Commission on the proposal that we have tabled. We are now moving on to technical dialogue in this process. We're going to be discussing specific areas. The proposal from Poland itself is specific in the sense that the situation of Poland is specific in the European Union and requires a separate solution. We are talking with the European Commission about the legal basis, the eligible costs, the support period, the instruments, how to solve debt issues, the investment issues, and finally the scale of the degressivity of this production. I would like these talks to allow us to draw up and notify this large law as soon as possible, which would address in a systematic manner the issue of support for the public aid of the coal mining sector that has been wound up. I want it to sound like it's here, too. Today, we are not talking about the restructuring of this sector, we are just talking about the liquidation of this sector, giving generous conditions for moving away from the hard mining work of mining workers. Therefore, I firmly believe that the Polish state, which coordinates Poland's energy policy, which is also responsible for the social issues of this process, should be generous here, should ensure that the workers who leave should be given adequate resources, and should finally provide a sufficient period of time to invest in new manufacturing units, no longer coal, and on the other hand to ensure the energy security of Poland. Because today, with regard to the basis, stability of the system, it is based on coal production blocks, and we have been in a situation in the last few weeks in a completely specific situation where these blocks work at a level that we haven't known for a long time. We also know that we are in a situation of completely absurd prices in Europe, absurdly high wholesale energy prices. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u304	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present an opinion on the draft law amending the law on the functioning of hard coal mining and certain other laws in parliamentary printing No 1503. The amendments to this Act are forced to further restructure the Polish hard coal mining sector for several reasons. For a long time, the stable functioning of the mining industry has been hindered by the difficult situation in the international coal market. From the fourth quarter of 2019 to April 2020, energy coal prices have steadily decreased. The main causes of price falls are high supply, low natural gas prices, a decline in the profitability of European coal-based energy, a long-standing SARS-CoV-2 pandemic, which is a decline in global economic activity and thus a decrease in demand for electricity. In addition, as a Member State of the European Union, we have commitments to adapt to the European Union's climate and energy policy, which is based on achieving the objectives set for decarbonising the energy sector, increasing the share of renewable sources in production and energy consumption, establishing and harmonising regional, trans-state energy markets. So here it is important to focus on the systematic increase in energy efficiency. I wanted to say that technological factors are also very important. We are also dealing with a situation where the increasing availability of gas fuel is important as an alternative to solid fuel, a reduction in investment and operating costs of RES, as well as the development of energy storage technologies. All of this causes coal energy to go into liquidation. According to the assumptions of Poland's energy policy until 2040. Poland has entered a state of energy transformation and is moving towards a zero-carbon economy through fair energy transformation, the development of RES, energy efficiency improvements, as I have already mentioned, and air quality. During the evolutionary transformation of the Polish energy sector it is necessary for the mining sector to ensure continuous supply of hard coal at competitive prices, and this means that the reduction of the mining industry must be carried out with a guarantee of the profitability of the sector, rational exploitation and distribution of the planned raw material. The bill is to some extent the implementation of the agreement concluded on 25 September 2020 between government representatives and the Inter-Union Protest and Strajkow Committee of the Silesian-Dąbrowski Region on the future functioning of the coal mining sector in Poland. It concerns the possibility to benefit from cover benefits, which is implemented in the draft law by extending the time limit within which employees of mining plants can benefit from them. The amendment of the Act is intended to allow for further acquisition of mines by SRK SA and their liquidation and restructuring of employment. The acquisition of minerals from existing companies of JSW SA and PGG SA and the financing of their liquidation from a budget grant will constitute authorised public aid, the scope of which is defined in the decision of the Council of the European Union of 10 December 2010. The costs of decommissioning of mines put into liquidation in 20212023 amount to PLN 2 billion 29 million. The Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice recommends that the High Sejm adopt an amendment to the law in parliamentary print No 1503. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u366	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The project provides for the implementation of e-invoices in Poland from January next year in voluntary form. How exactly will the e-invoice work? Invoices will be generated in the business accounting system and sent to counterparties via the Ministry's database. Both the supplier and the buyer will see an e-invoice in their free account in the National e-Factur System, which is our application. What does it look like in other countries of the European Union? E-invoice is a complete technological novelty. Poland will be the fourth country in the European Union to implement the e-invoice. In 2017, Italy implemented it, a year later it implemented Spain, in 2019 - Portugal. Thanks to the betting on technologies, thanks to the Big Data analyses that concern the trading of goods and flows between companies, the period of activities of tax offenders and, above all, the period associated with the detection of their activities by the State has been significantly reduced: from the few years we have seen before 2015 to only a few months. Now thanks to e-invoice we will analyze invoices not in the amplitude of time 1.5 months from the date of their issue, as it is now, but practically in real time. Is the e-invoice safe? We are in a very comfortable position because - although we are not a pioneer - we are not an experimenter. On the contrary, we are based on the experiences of other countries. During the process of creating new regulations, we were in contact with them and based directly on the technological regulations and solutions implemented in them. The main reference point for Polish regulations was the Italian solutions and the process followed by Italy in its contacts with the European Commission in connection with the need to confirm the safety of this tool and its implementation. New e-government services and new technologies are a key issue in the implementation of e-invoices. This is, you can say, a global trend, but certainly a trend visible in the European Union. France has just recognised the implementation of e-invoices as a sine qua non condition for maintaining a low level of VAT gap, in Poland it wants to implement e-invoices in a voluntary formula from January next year. We want the e-invoice to start working as of 2023. Yes, France is two more years behind us. Germany is just debating the implementation of this solution. The German equivalent of NIK recommended the implementation of e-invoices in the Federal Republic of Germany. Germany, if they implement it, it's more like a five-year perspective. So we're half a decade ahead of our Western neighbours. E-invoice is a very good tool for sealing the tax system for the National Tax Administration, but above all it introduces a very large simplification in the field of business activities. One might ask what exactly the e-invoice provides for companies. First of all, and most importantly, it's safe. The invoice issued in the Entrepreneur's system, sent and recorded in the Ministry of Finance system will remain in the MF database, so the Entrepreneur will not have to store it himself. The invoice will never be destroyed, it will not be lost, there will be no need to issue duplicates, and you will not have to wait for confirmation of receipt of the invoice by the counterparty, because both parties of the transaction will immediately see it in the system. Secondly, it provides comfort. Now each invoice looks different, the key information for companies is contained in different locations. The e-invoice will be a structured document. What does that mean? This means it will be very comfortable, because it will always have the same format. You will not have to look on the invoice of important data, e.g. commodity prices or the VAT rates, because always the information will be in the same place. Finally, it allows for a faster return of VAT. Using the e-invoice will allow you to obtain a VAT refund more quickly. Thanks to e-invoice, the entrepreneur is transparent, becomes a trusted taxpayer, and that is why we want him to start treating him like that right away. Taxpayers who choose an e-invoice will receive a refund of VAT within a period of 1/3 shorter than the statutory provisions. The deadline will therefore be reduced by 20 days, not 60 days, but 40 days. In view of the above, I kindly ask the High House to accept the project and give it a further run. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u371	Thank you very much, sir. Mr Tomasz Trela, parliamentary club of the Left. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u372	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I am pleased to present the direction and preliminary position of the Coalition Parliamentary Club of the Left as regards the government draft law amending the Act on the Tax on Goods and Services and some other laws, print No 1547. This proposal proposes amendments to the Act of 11 March 2004 on VAT on goods and services, hereinafter referred to as the VAT Act, as regards the introduction of the National e-Factur System and solutions aimed at further simplifying the taxation of goods and services by taxable persons. These changes concern simplifications in the issuing of corrective invoices, including a reduction in the number of elements of such invoices, advance invoices and the optional use of duplicate invoices. The draft also provides for amendments to the Act of 29 August 1997 - Tax Order and the Act of 16 November 2016 on National Tax Administration. The solution introduced, the National e-Factur System, is optional. The proposed law regulates the possibility of issuing invoices structured as one of the authorised forms of documenting transactions alongside paper invoices and current electronic invoices. The solution adopted will allow the issuer of invoices to exercise the right to tax preferences provided for in this draft law - i.e. The 40-day tax refund deadline and the settlement of the in-minus adjustment for the period of issue of the correction invoice - also in the absence of the consent of the recipient of the structured invoice to receive it in the National e-Factur System. In addition, the National e-Factur System will not be able to issue invoices for which separate provisions provide for a lack of obligation to include the number by which the seller is identified for tax purposes or data enabling the buyer to be identified unambiguously. Similarly, the term "pro forma invoice' is customary and does not appear in the provisions of the VAT Act. The issuing of a pro forma invoice does not have any effect on VAT, i.e. the obligation to pay the tax shown in this document, nor does it constitute a basis for the counterparty to deduct the VAT shown in that document. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, the Left Coalition Parliamentary Club expresses its willingness and willingness to work on this document. This is the first reading of this project. The project has been addressed, it will be addressed to the finance committee. There are specific questions to be asked. Perhaps this project should be clarified or detailed. However, undoubtedly the introduction of such an e-invoice system is a step in the right direction. I am, of course, concerned when I hear that the Minister is saying that we are one of the precursors, whether you will be able to deal with the introduction of this system, whether it will operate from 1 January. But I hope that our doubts will be resolved to some extent in the finance committee. Indeed, many comments were received by the Ministry of Finance and they were also taken into account during the next months of work on the project. In August, the invoice scheme was made available to entrepreneurs. Since October, i.e. almost two weeks from now, tests will begin with entrepreneurs concerning the transmission of invoices and the transparency of the system. As of January, regulations will be introduced to allow the optional transmission of invoices and this functionality will also be opened. Since January 2023, i.e. after 12 months of voluntary operation of this solution, this will become a mandatory solution for all market players. It is also a procedure which results from consultations with the European Commission, which also recommends voluntary piloting and subsequently widespread implementation. Exactly the same way this project was carried out in Italy, which also first bet on voluntaryity and at least one year of piloting directly with entrepreneurs. So this is where we're going through the pavement. We are in current technical contact with the countries that have already implemented this solution, and with the European Commission, with which legal regulations and technical issues are also consulted. As far as authorisation is concerned, here we provide for broad forms of authorisation, both a trusted profile and a qualified signature or a qualified stamp. With regard to the purpose of e-invoice, I would like to stress that the most important thing is not 40 days to return. The most important issue is the comfort and security of the taxpayer and, above all, the speed of trading. Quick access to information that the invoice was received by the other party, that it was accepted, and, finally, the security against VAT carousels and this danger that still threatens taxpayers, namely being dragged into criminal activity by entities that are merely pretending to be entrepreneurs, and in fact turn goods only on paper. With regard to the question of sealing the tax system, once again I would like to stress that in the last 5 years the VAT gap has decreased in Poland as soon as possible in the European Union. She fell almost three times from 25%. in 2015 to just over 9% in 2019. Let us remember that in 2015 25% of the VAT gap meant that every fourth of the zloty, which should be paid by VAT-people to the state budget, was either in the grey zone or in the pocket of the VAT-based mafia. In 2019 it was already every 10 zlotys. On the other hand, we also noticed a very interesting pattern. In the pandemic year 2020, despite the closure of the economy, despite the difficulties in the operation of tax offices and the National Tax Administration, the VAT gap has decreased again. According to CASE data, the report prepared for the European Commission decreased year to year 1/5 to 8.2% , i.e. 1/4 lower than the EU average, twice lower than the VAT gap in Slovakia, four times smaller than e.g. Romania. According to the calculations of the Ministry of Finance, the state budget gains about PLN 25 billion each year on VAT sealing. These are measures that wouldn't have been budgeted otherwise if it hadn't been for sealing tools to fight tax mobs. It is also 25 billion PLN, it is huge money that would go into the pockets of criminals as a result of VAT fraud, if not for the steps taken in recent years by the Ministry of Finance. You could ask yourself how this success has been achieved. Here you spoke very much about the 2015 plans, but what else are the plans, and what else is the implementation. We are talking about very concrete steps here. Poland is the first country of the European Union to implement a very coherent plan to fight tax crime. The plan actually consists of four basic steps and four functionalities. Firstly, it is now more difficult to organise tax merry-go-rounds, VAT fraud. It is practically not possible to register at the office of the company a post office, a company with only and only a mailbox, because each application is verified by the tax administration on the spot and by telephone. Secondly, the duration of the VAT carousel is getting shorter. Minister, let me remind you that we're talking about invoices. Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Finance Jan Sarnowski: The company artificially rotates invoices not for several years, but for a few months at most, and after the implementation of the e-invoice it will only be a few weeks. In conclusion, the e-invoice will lead to the fact that the analyst carried out in the Ministry of Finance will rapidly accelerate again. Just as the revolution was the implementation of the analysis of JPK files, and not just collecting them - this has allowed us to accelerate the detection of VAT carousels from years to months, and that is a few months, not a dozen - so now by analysing invoices, we will be able to detect tax offenders on an ongoing basis in just a few weeks. The result will be that we will still be able to reduce the VAT gap. As is now the VAT gap - according to preliminary figures for 2020 - about 8.2% , so in the next 3 or 4 years we will be able to reach the level of France, i.e. one of the leaders of the fair VAT system in the European Union, namely the level of 5%. If I haven't answered any of the questions, we'll answer them in writing. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u393	Thank you very much, Minister. Ladies and gentlemen, the discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u394	I have the honour to present on behalf of the Polish Coalition Club our position on the government draft law amending the Act on Tax on Goods and Services and some other laws, print No 1547. This draft law concerns the introduction of the National e-Factur System, KSeF, and solutions aimed at simplifying tax treatment for goods and services by taxpayers. These changes concern simplifications in the issuing of corrective invoices, including a reduction in the number of elements of such invoices, advance invoices and the optional use of duplicate invoices. The solutions will contribute to strengthening checks on the regularity of VAT clearance and simplifying the billing process for traders by remote monitoring of the turnover of invoices by tax authorities and will enable the revenue of the State budget to be increased as a result of the assumed increase in VAT collection at each stage of trade in goods and services to be increased. The e-invoice will be voluntary at first and will become mandatory from 2023. High Izbo! The implementation of e-invoices is another example of the digitalisation of fiscal services. It will have two very positive effects on the Polish economy. Firstly, it will make it much easier to account for business-to-business transactions. Secondly, it will speed up the detection of attempts to extort tax, thereby increasing the security of honest companies and reducing the VAT gap. You cannot ignore the advantages of the proposed solutions, such as: speed - taxpayers choosing an e-invoice will receive a refund of VAT in a period of 1/3 shorter, the return date will be reduced for them by 20 days, from 60 days to 40 days; security - invoice will remain in the MF database and will never be destroyed or lost, there will be no need to issue duplicates; acceleration of turnover - thanks to operation through the Ministry base we will always be sure that the invoice has been delivered to the contractor; convenience - e-invoices will be issued according to a single pattern, so that it will be very easy to use; standardisation - mutual settlement and booking of invoices in FK systems will become much easier; less obligations - the taxpayer will not have to store invoices issued in KSeF because they will be kept by the administration for a period of 10 years, and thus as a rule in the period during which most tax obligations are made a statute; less data to be sent - the taxpayer issuing invoices in KSeF will not have to send invoices to the tax authorities on demand of the Single Control File structure for invoices, JPK FA. These data will be available to tax authorities in KSeF and therefore their additional transmission would not be justified. E-invoice is a lot of business benefits. This is not only a quicker refund of VAT, but also an acceleration and standardization of the invoicing process proposed by entrepreneurs. In addition, it is the elimination of many tax obligations, including in particular the need for the taxpayer to store invoices and make them available when checking or transferring the bodies of the JPK FA structure on request. Poland is the fourth country in the European Union to introduce an e-invoice. Spain, Portugal and Italy have introduced such a solution, France has set a deadline for 2023. Polish regulations were mainly based on the Italian model, where e-invoices were made available already in 2017, and after a transitional period it is mandatory since 2019. The objectives of introducing e-invoices are legitimate, but it is hard to resist the impression that e-invoices will be another tool of the Ministry of Finance to control tax settlements. Tax authorities will have more accurate information on the circulation of invoices in the economy, and this brings many unknowns. It is therefore impossible, High Izbo, not to ask questions on matters that experts report indicating doubts about the project: How will data be transferred between the Central National e-Factur System, KSeF and taxpayers' billing systems? Who will be responsible for archiving documents and possible loss of documents? Who will be responsible for the technical problems of the system or for the mistakes of people issuing invoices? In addition, e-invoicers will have to use an IT system that they do not have control over, e.g. They can't protect him from data leaks or hacker attacks on their own. This, too, is an important problem that should sound in committee work. High Izbo! On behalf of the Koalicja Polska Club, I request further work on this draft law in the parliamentary committees.

[[The Gathered Rise Up]] The resolution of the Polish Sejm of 17 September 2021 on the celebration of Jarogniew Drwaski - President of Poznań in 1919 and 1921 on the 100th anniversary of death. On 14 September 2021 the 100th anniversary of the death of Jarogniew Drwaski - a lawyer, social activist, swordsman of the idea of independence of Poland and Polishness of Wielkopolska, the first Polish president of Poznań during the Second Polish Republic. Jarogniew Drzęski, born on December 6, 1875 in Glinno near Wągrowiec in the land family, has been associated with Poznan his whole life. He was a graduate of the Poznań Junior High School. Mary Magdalene. After law studies in Berlin, he opened a law firm in Poznań. It belonged to the last generation of great organics. He was the President of the Association of Legal and Economics in Poznań and the Association of Singing Circles, as well as co-founded Polish sports organizations in Wielkopolska. He was politically involved, he was a member of the conservative National Union, he was on the benches of the Poznań City Council. During World War I he was one of the organizers of secret civil committees which prepared a plan to take over power in Wielkopolska by Poles. After the outbreak of the revolution in Germany in November 1918, Polish environments moved Jarogniew Drwaski to the position of the commissar's chief mayor of Poznań. After the outbreak of the Wielkopolska Uprising and the mastery of Wielkopolska by the insurgents Jarogniew Drzewski was elected president of the city of Poznań in elections to local authorities. As the President of Poznań Jarogniew Drzewski, he deserved especially in the fields of effective approximation of the city and the organisation of public works in order to counter unemployment and at the same time to colonise administration. Jarogniew Drwaski's great contribution was to support the initiative of establishing the University of Poznań in May 1919. He was at the head of the committee, which organized the I Targ Poznański - an undertaking that eventually turned into the International Poznań Fair. The nationwide recognition was that he was elected President of the Polish Towns Association in 1920. The hard work for Poland and Poznań has marked President Drewski's health. He died on 14 September 1921 in Poznań when he was less than 46 years old. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the 100th anniversary of the death of President Jarogniew Drzewski pays tribute to him and believes that he has served Poland well with his long-term work in favour of Polishness Wielkopolska. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u416	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! During the second reading of the government draft law amending the Act - Construction Law and the Act on Planning and Spatial Development from 1534 and 1544 prints, an application was submitted for rejection of the bill and 11 amendments. The Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy have today considered both the proposal and the amendments. High Izbo! The reasons for the rejection request are not true. The project is not a threat to spatial governance. It does not deprive the municipalities of planning power, because the construction must be in line with the local spatial planning plan, and in the absence of it, it is the municipality which makes decisions about the conditions of construction and land development. It can therefore be presumed that it will be issued in line with its own spatial policy and infrastructure development policy. Moreover, it is not true to say that this law will cause the extension of buildings, because the changes which are contained in the article. 2, on the clarification of the principles of urban analysis, will counter the spill-over of buildings. This has been borne in mind by the joint infrastructure and self-government committees which recommend the High House, firstly, rejecting the motion for rejection of the bill, secondly, rejecting all the amendments tabled and, third, of course, adopting the whole draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
