I would like to ask the President of the Supreme Audit Chamber of Marian Banas to present a report.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-17-sejm-37-3.u428	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In accordance with the constitutional and statutory obligation, I present a report on the activities of the Supreme Audit Chamber in 2020 to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland approved by the College of NIK. As the primary body of state control, whose reports provide reliable state information, we are based on values and principles that we have been faithful to for more than 100 years: impartiality, integrity and devotion to the service of the state and society. The Supreme Audit Chamber shall carry out its tasks on the basis of the annual work plan adopted by the College of NIK and submitted to the Sejm. High Izbo! The Supreme Audit Chamber, in carrying out its tasks, completed 91 topics of planned controls in 2020, including the control of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy objectives. We also carried out ad hoc inspection procedures on 63 subjects. The NIK carried out a total of 2007 unit controls in 1629 entities and formulated 4548 post-control applications. In addition, we have submitted 122 proposals for de lege ferenda. We've filed 55 reports of possible crimes or offences with law enforcement. We also sent notices to spokespersons of public finance discipline. The tangible effect of our work is the financial results of the audit. The amount spent in violation of the law is more than PLN 345 million. the amounts spent following infringement actions are more than PLN 136 million and the financial benefits are almost PLN 12 million. Let the fact that 5577 complaints, requests and requests for checks have only been received in the past year show that there is ever increasing confidence in the Supreme Audit Chamber. The applicants were mainly private individuals, but they were also MEPs, senators, councillors and social and trade union organisations. As many as 209 cases have been examined and planned to be used in the audit. A number of others supported the design of control activities, while all of them were analysed and supported the information base of the Supreme Audit Chamber. High Seym! The Supreme Audit Chamber published and formulated 609 follow-up proposals in 2020 more than in the previous year. The report on the activities of the Supreme Audit Chamber in 2020 presents the more important findings of the audit, broken down by six areas of operation of the State. In the area of economic security, NIK controlled inter alia. issues related to the financial system, financial stability and tax system. The control of public debt management and the liquidity of public finance units has proven to be justified by the increased burden on public finances caused by the prevention of the epidemic. NIK also addressed the problem of supervision of companies with the participation of the State Treasury, the purchasing procedures carried out by these companies or the management of the property of the State Treasury. It also assessed the management of the capital and investment portfolio of the Industrial Development Agency SA and related entities. It also assessed the stability of the economy and the conditions for its development. I would like to stress that in the above area NIK has been monitoring the state of Polish energy and security of energy supply in Poland for years, which has been particularly reflected in the control the effectiveness of the expenditure incurred by electricity producers. On the other hand, transport controls focused on transport infrastructure, system organisation and development of sustainable transport. We also studied electromobility for the first time. In the area of safe, efficient and effective NIK has checked, among others. the implementation of development work for the Ministry of National Defence. Audits on the functioning of services were important, especially in crisis situations. The COVID-19 epidemic has determined to a large extent the issues of our health control and ensuring a decent standard of living. In this area, we have examined issues related to the functioning of the health system. In view of the social situation, we have checked the welfare homes or the feeding of children and young people during the epidemic. In environmental and climate areas, controls focused on testing the effects of pollutants on human health, the impact of human activities on nature and the assessment of environmental projects. The Supreme Audit Chamber also examined issues of care for education, culture and national heritage. We consider it particularly important that the educational offer is not adapted to the labour market. In the field of research, NIK pointed to the weakness of supervision of the implementation of research projects. High Izbo! In its analysis of the implementation of the State budget and monetary policy objectives, the Supreme Audit Chamber considered, with regard to the State budget as a constitutional value, the need to restore the State budget of an appropriate rank related to its specific nature and central position in the public finance system. The reasons for this position were the finding of a significant financial imbalance of the State in 2020, which was caused inter alia. planning and carrying out major financial operations leading to an increase in public debt, excluding the State budget. These included, in particular, operations involving the transfer of tax bonds and lending to different entities in 2020 instead of grants which would increase government spending and deficit by almost PLN 30 billion. In addition, numerous financial operations resulting in an increase in the State's liabilities, which merely apparently did not increase public debt according to national methodology, were also to be provided for a reduction in the state budget. An example of this was, among others, the practice. issuance of bonds of the Polish Development Fund SA and Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego for the Anti-Action Fund COVID-19 with a total value of more than PLN 166 billion, whose repayment and service is guaranteed by the State Treasury. The Supreme Audit Chamber therefore notes with concern the further devaluation of the importance of the state budget resulting in the possibility of showing any result in the budget of the State, thus no longer relevant for the assessment of the financial situation of the State. In this respect, as President of the Supreme Audit Chamber and as well as the former Finance Minister, I would like to point out that the state budget is a fundamental instrument of governance and thus political power, and therefore sets limits in the State of law for the exercise of this power by the various authorities and public authorities' agendas, including government administration. I'm sorry. High Izbo! The Supreme Audit Chamber attaches particular importance to cooperation with the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. Each year, it analyses and takes into account suggestions for audit topics submitted by the Sejm authorities to the NIK's work plan. The results of the checks carried out The Supreme Audit Chamber presents to the Sejm and its authorities. In the past year, it submitted 112 information on the results of the monitoring of ad hoc plans, 99 information on the results of the audit of the implementation of the State budget, analysis of the implementation of the State budget and monetary policy objectives, activity report of NIK in 2019. I would like to point out that the Supreme Audit Chamber is functioning efficiently and effectively, despite the fact that the NIK College is part-member. The fact that the new members of the NIK College have not been appointed by the Marshal of the Sejm is of great concern despite my submission of 27 proposals on this matter. Since becoming President, I have submitted six letters to the Marshals of the Sejm to complement the composition of the college. Unfortunately, the Marshal of the Sejm has appointed only seven members of the NIK College. High Seym! Supreme Audit Chamber is also an internationally recognised and valued partner. NIK is selected for international peer reviews teams in other highest audit bodies and successfully competes in competitions for external auditor and in competitions for the implementation of projects of twin cooperation of the European Commission. As President of the Supreme Audit Chamber, I was again elected Vice-Chairman of the European Organisation of Supreme Audit Authorities EUROSAI, bringing together 50 countries in the region and the European Court of Auditors. In addition, I represent the Supreme Audit Chamber on the board of the International Organization of Supreme Audit Authorities of INTOSAI, which brings together 201 members worldwide. This recognition for NIK allows to have a real impact on the development of modern state control in the world, among others. through participation in the creation of international control standards. The Supreme Audit Chamber is also chairing the work of the EUROSAI Working Group on environmental control, where it carries out, inter alia, International control on the management of plastic waste, and INTOSAI Sub-Committee on internal control standards where it is responsible for developing these international standards. NIK is implementing twin cooperation projects in Kosovo, Jordan and Palestine on its own. Such projects enable us to share our experience in digitisation of the control process, ensuring the quality of control and compliance of the control procedure with international standards of public control, also in order to promote independence and objectivity in controls. Currently, NIK is also conducting an external audit of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development of the OECD, to which it was selected through an open competition. The NIK shall also have its representatives in the College of External Audits of the European Union's financing mechanism for the European Union's military operations ATHENA, the ESA European Space Agency's audit committee and the European Organisation for Foreign Affairs. EUROCONTROL Air Navigation Safety. High Seym! Last year for the Supreme Audit Chamber it is also a time of extremely hard and effective work in implementing organisational improvements, as well as improvements in control processes. In 2020 the first strategy of the Supreme Audit Chamber in 18 years was adopted, 2021 and2024, with the adoption of which we started to set up an integrated monitoring support platform to reduce control time in the control unit, improve control quality and ultimately increase its dynamics. The strategy assumes, among other things, improving substantive and methodological competences by improving the professional workshop of controllers, strong support for the process of control of digital technologies, updating the control methodology. The Supreme Audit Chamber (Bell) focused on updating and developing information systems for both functional, technological and performance purposes. The systems currently in use support the NIK work plan and contain control data and support and monitor the control process. We have been preparing for more than a year to digitise the control process in the Supreme Audit Chamber. Designed analytical and reporting system will support the work of controllers, among others. through a multi-dimensional analysis of data from different sources. We also introduce digital modeling and management of workflow paths and run performance measurements of control processes. We are also working on modernising the control methodology based on current knowledge and the wider use of risk assessment, and a new manual of the controller, the previous edition of which took place almost 20 years ago, remains in the study. Action was successfully carried out in 2020 in the Supreme Audit Chamber to centralise the implementation of its budget. Since 1 January 2021, the budget of the Supreme Audit Chamber has been implemented only at the level of the component manager, with tangible results of both financial and organisational nature. The centralisation of accounting and the planning and execution of public contracts has been carried out. As a result, the operating costs of NIK were reduced by reducing employment in financial and accounting services from a total of 54 to 30 people. Further savings resulted in centralisation of the planning and execution of public contracts. As a result of centralisation, the effectiveness of financial management of NIK has been increased, better supervision of budget implementation processes has been ensured and organisational improvements have been possible, which has resulted in the creation of savings that have been earmarked for the performance of statutory tasks. The strategy succeeded in the idea of setting up a new department of ad hoc control coordination in the Supreme Audit Chamber, accompanied by the need to simplify procedures for the taking up and implementation of ad hoc controls. The efficiency of the department is demonstrated by the fact that in the last year it has coordinated and carried out or carried out nine complex checks covering inspection activities in 22 units. It is defined as the foundation of energy security of our country, because it produces 1/4 of energy in Poland. 12 energy blocks are currently being exploited. The power plant emits quite a lot of greenhouse gases, but it is the least active when it comes to CO emissions Forsaking sophisticated environmental standards in Brussels without understanding the Polish local specificity can lead to conflict against this background. The proceedings concerning the issue of building conditions are the longest, often exceeding the deadline of 100 days, and the main reason for such dragging procedures is staff shortages. It is clear that, in this situation, local authorities will not have the opportunity or the means to adapt to the new requirements regarding the deadlines for issuing decisions, which will result in the imposition of sanctions on them by the voivodships. Alternatively, they will have to increase employment, which also has financial implications. The fiscal negative effect on local governments is therefore evident. The aim of the project was to organise this area, and in fact it contains rules that are dead or which can easily be avoided, which will aggravate the chaos in urban planning and we cannot allow that to happen. These are just a few controversial assumptions that require concrete changes. In the interest of a slight simplification of procedures, it is not possible to reduce the security standards for the implementation of investments. In Poland there are already known cases of even fatal accidents caused (The Bell) by the wrong construction of temporary buildings. At least indicate the sensitive stages of investment in which professional supervision would continue to be mandatory. I have provided the Minister of Infrastructure in the form of interpellation with detailed provisions that raise doubts as to the appropriateness of their introduction, and I hope that the revision of this area will be carried out correctly, and the proposed provisions will change in order to prevent the deterioration of the chaos in urban planning. High Seym! PGL State Forests are the largest organisation in the European Union managing forests belonging to the Treasury. The organisation of State Forests, which has been perfected for decades, is based on the principle of self-financing possible through the existence of so-called. Forest Fund. That's why the Forests don't use taxpayers' money. Forest units operate at national, regional and local level. State Forests employ 25,000. persons. They are the ninth largest employer in Poland. In Poland, about 375 thousand people work in forestry-related industries. people, i.e. on average every 40 Pole working. Polish foresters are not only working and caring for forests, it is also their passion. It is mandatory and appropriate to carry out its mission of State Forests - forest management on behalf of society, reconciling the needs of nature and man in many dimensions: natural, social and economic, as well as environmental humanism. They manage on 1/4 of the Polish area, a particularly valuable part, because forests grow on it. More importantly, the part that is systematically growing, because the efforts of foresters are increasing! Foresters acquire wood within the limits set by environmental standards, scientific research and 10-year plans for forest equipment approved by the Minister for the Environment. The State Forests acquire wood, but at the same time they renovate the stands and forest their former wasteland. Every year, foresters plant as many as 500 million different species of new trees, or on average 57 thousand. for an hour. Thus, they prove that meeting our demand for wood and ensuring the sustainability of forests are not conflicting interests! So wood in Poland is a natural wealth, which is completely renewable! This is the impossible that Polish foresters are doing! We're getting over 30 million meters. Poland is at the forefront of the European forest area. Currently, the forest area in our country is over 9.2 million ha, which corresponds to the forest density of 29.6%. The vast majority are state forests, of which about 7.3 million ha are managed by the State Forests State Farm. In Poland, forests continue to arrive. The country's Lesity was increased from 21% in 1945 to 29.6% presently. The foundation of forest works is the National Foresting Programme. In 2020, the forests occupied about 30% of the country's surface and in 2050 they were expected to occupy 33%. As I mentioned, the State Forests carry out their tasks and objectives in a multidimensional manner. Thus they include reserves as separate areas with specific natural values, landscape parks created in areas with exceptional natural and aesthetic and landscape value. They also organise natural monuments, protection zones set up to protect the sites and sites of plants or fungi protected by species or species conservation, breeding sites and the regular presence of animals protected by species. Further, ecological pastures - charming and somewhat secret places in the form of small water mesh, interpolated streams of trees and shrubs, peat bogs, swamps and dunes. State Forests also take their own conservation initiatives. They help selected species of animals and plants to settle places where these species are extinct - re-inductions or where they are threatened with extinction - restitutions. I would add that national parks are in an excellent part supported financially from the abovementioned LP Forest Fund. PGL State Forests are an active part of the EU's sustainable economy in the European Green Deal (the European Green Deal), mainly in the task of combating biodiversity loss and reducing pollution levels. Hunting is an integral part of protecting the natural environment. In addition to hunters for forest animals, foresters also take care of foresters. There are 207 hunting circuits in their territory. It is made up of 141 animal breeding centres (OHZ). Foresters are involved in determining the size of forest game, which is necessary for the proper management of animal populations. Payment of very significant amounts of compensation while halting commercial hunting (e.g. for foreigners) leads to the bankruptcy of hunting circles and the resignation of many hunters. In my view, these issues need to be addressed urgently. Hunters and foresters, together with the State Veterinary Inspection, cooperate in preventing the spread of African swine fever (ASF) in the population of wild boars living in our forests and thus help protect domestic swine flocks from ASF virus.
